I think the cleanest rigorous route: rely on GT24's definitions and their "mirror pair" criterion, and prove the theorem by:

Theorem A (lattice arithmetic, proved): The Dolgachev mirror of the lattice H (relative to Λ) is H ⊕ E8(-1) ⊕ E8(-1).

Theorem B (fibration structure, proved): A very general K3 Y with primitive quasipolarization by H⊕E8(-1)⊕E8(-1) (i.e., NS(Y) = H⊕E8⊕E8) carries a unique elliptic fibration π: Y → P^1 (up to automorphism) with two fibers of type II* and four fibers of type I1; its discriminant locus in P^1 has six points; there is an allowable loop γ (in the sense of [GT24, Def 4.11]) encircling exactly the two II* fibers; and the quasipolarization is compatible with (π, γ) in the sense of [GT24, Def 4.21]. — Prove as much as possible; cite Saint-Donat, Kodaira, Shioda.

Theorem C (degeneration side, cite GT24 + ABE22): Let 𝔛 → Δ be a Tyurin degeneration of K3 surfaces which is H-polarized in the corrected sense and very general. Then: (a) each V_i is a rational elliptic surface with double curve an anticanonical fiber; (b) the monodromy-invariant lattice L = H ⊂ H^2(X_t) identifies with NS(X_t) (very general interior point... well at least NS ⊇ H and the boundary point is general); (c) the essential mirror data: the "restricted/paired" lattice of the degeneration (in GT24's sense: L ⊕ E8(V1) ⊕ E8(V2)) is H ⊕ E8 ⊕ E8.

Then [GT24, Def 5.1 mirror-pair criterion] reduces the theorem to: matching of essential lattice data + genericity: both sides very general ⇒ the mirror pairing holds.

**§2 Lattice lemmas** (full proofs):

Lemma 2.1: Let Λ = U^3 ⊕ E8(-1)^2. (a) H^⊥ ∩ Λ for any primitive embedding H ↪ Λ is ≅ U^2 ⊕ E8(-1)^2. (b) If S ⊂ Λ is primitive and unimodular then Λ = S ⊕ S^⊥ (internal orthogonal sum) and S^⊥ is even unimodular of signature (3−s+, 19−s−). Proof: unimodular S: discr trivial: S = S^{⊥⊥}∩... standard: for unimodular S: the intersection form gives a splitting: Λ = S ⊕ S^⊥: proof: the inclusion S ↪ Λ ⊂ Λ^∨: since S = S^∨: for x ∈ Λ: define functional s ↦ (x, s) ∈ S^∨ = S: corresponds to s0 ∈ S: x − s0 ∈ S^⊥. ✓. Even unimodular: complement of even unimodular in even unimodular is even unimodular: discr(S^⊥) ≅ discr(S)^op-ish = 0 ✓; even: S^⊥ ⊂ Λ even ✓.

**§3 The mirror lattice.** Define (following Dolgachev [Dol96] and as used in [GT24, §5]): the mirror of an M-polarized K3... For our degeneration setting, define the mirror polarization lattice via the Type II data: 

Theorem 3.1: Let 𝔛 → Δ be a Tyurin degeneration of K3 surfaces which is H-polarized in the sense of (corrected) Def 3.20. Let L ≅ H denote the monodromy-invariant sublattice, T := L^⊥ ∩ Λ ≅ U^2 ⊕ E8(-1)^2 (via any marking identifying L with the standard H ⊂ Λ), and let ζ ∈ T be the (primitive isotropic) monodromy class (image of the monodromy N on H^2(X_t); see §5). Then the lattice K(𝔛) := (ζ^⊥ ∩ T)/Zζ is independent of choices up to isometry and K(𝔛) ≅ H ⊕ E8(-1) ⊕ E8(-1). Proof: Cor 2.5. Independence: choices: the identification T ≅ U^2⊕E8^2 (via Eichler etc.) fine.

Title: "Mirror symmetry for Tyurin degenerations of H-polarized K3 surfaces: proof of the main theorem"

Sections:
1. Introduction & statement (Theorem 1.2). Notation. Remarks on the corrected positivity condition and its role (Remark 1.3).
2. Lattices. Lemmas 2.1–2.5 with proofs (unimodular splitting; classification; Eichler; the key computation Corollary: K(ζ) ≅ H⊕E8^2).
3. Lattice-polarized K3 surfaces and Dolgachev duality. Prop 3.1: mirror lattice of H is H⊕E8^2 (two computations: Dolgachev discr form; and degeneration-based (ζ^⊥∩T)/Zζ). Prop: very general H-polarized: NS = H, T = U^2⊕E8^2. Very general H⊕E8^2: NS = H⊕E8^2, T = U^2.
4. The degeneration side: semistability, monodromy N and ζ ∈ T primitive isotropic (Lemma 4.1, cited+proved bits); components RES (Lemma 4.2, cited Kulikov/PP/Friedman + role of positivity); the essential lattice K(𝔛) ≅ H⊕E8^2 (Thm 4.3 — the (ζ^⊥∩T)/Zζ computation, complete proof).
5. The fibration side: Prop 5.1: very general Y with NS = H⊕E8^2: existence of compatible elliptic fibration (cite [GT24]); complete analysis: fiber class f, section, Δ = E8^2, two II* fibers, four I1, MW = 0 (proofs via Shioda–Tate, Kodaira). Lemma 5.2: allowable loop around the two II* fibers (cite [GT24]); Lemma 5.3: compatibility (cite [GT24] + verify lattice-level content).
6. Assembly: Theorem 6.1 = main theorem: verify [GT24, Def 5.1] conditions: (i) lattice duality H ↔ H⊕E8^2 (Thm 4.3 + Prop 3.1); (ii) type data match (Type II monodromy ζ ↔ the loop γ data; cite [GT24, §5] correspondence theorem: for very general data these are equivalent); (iii) very generality. Conclude. ∎
7. Bibliography: the two required + Serre, Nikulin, Dolgachev, Huybrechts, Saint-Donat, Friedman, Kulikov/PP, Kodaira, Schütt–Shioda or Miranda, maybe Morrison.

Lemma 2.1: Let S be an even unimodular lattice and S ⊂ Λ primitive with S unimodular. Then Λ = S ⊕ S^⊥ and S^⊥ is even unimodular of signature (λ+ − s+, λ− − s−). Proof: (i) For x ∈ Λ, the functional φ_x: S → Z, s ↦ (x,s): since S unimodular: ∃! s_x ∈ S with (s_x, s') = (x, s') ∀s': then x − s_x ∈ S^⊥: so Λ = S + S^⊥; S ∩ S^⊥ = 0 (radical of S trivial) ⟹ direct sum ✓. (ii) unimodular complement: discr(S^⊥) ≅ discr(S) = 0: standard: A_Λ = 0 = A_S ⊕ A_{S^⊥} (for orthogonal sum; for the internal primitive situation: A_{S^⊥} ≅ q(S^⊥) and Λ/S⊕S^⊥ finite with pairing linking: since A_Λ = 0 and A_S = 0: A_{S^⊥} = 0 ✓). Even: S^⊥ ⊂ Λ even ✓ (sublattice of even). Signature clear. ∎

Lemma 4.1 (monodromy of a Tyurin degeneration). Let 𝔛 → Δ be a semistable degeneration of K3 surfaces with central fibre X0 = V1 ∪ V2 (Tyurin). Then: (i) monodromy T ∈ O(H²(X_t,Z)) is unipotent, T = exp(N), N ≠ 0, N² = 0; (ii) N is an integral, symmetric endomorphism; (iii) there is a primitive isotropic class ζ ∈ H²(X_t,Z), unique up to sign, with N(v) = ±(v,ζ)ζ; in particular ker N = ζ^⊥ and im N = Zζ. Proof: (i) [PP81, §?]: semistable ⟹ unipotent (monodromy theorem); Type II (two components) ⟹ N ≠ 0 and N² = 0 [PP81]/[Fri83]. Hmm: N² = 0 for Type II: justification: the weight filtration: W_2 = ker N: rank ker N = 21 for Type II: cite [PP81, Theorem ?]: the weight filtration of a Type II degeneration has W_1 = im N of rank 1: so rank im N = 1 and N² = 0 ✓. (ii) N symmetric: standard [Griffiths]/Clemens–Schmid: cite [PS75? or via Huy16? hmm]. I'll cite "the monodromy theorem and the symmetry of N (see e.g. [Fri83, §4] or [PP81])". (iii) proved above (my linear algebra lemma — include full proof as Lemma 4.2 maybe: rank-1 symmetric nilpotent endomorphisms of unimodular lattices). ✓

Then: ζ ∈ L^⊥: N|_L = 0 (monodromy invariance of L): (ℓ, ζ) = 0 ∀ℓ ✓.

N(Λ) = Zζ means the SET {φ(x)ζ} = {nζ : n ∈ φ(Λ)Z} = Zζ ⟺ φ(Λ) = Z ✓): so φ surjective ✓ ⟹ η primitive ✓ ⟹ ζ = ±η primitive ✓✓ (in the Lemma's proof η = ±ζ). So ζ primitive in Λ ✓ automatic! And ζ ∈ T = L^⊥: primitive in T ✓ (argued) ✓.

Suppose (·,η) and (·,ζ₀) linearly independent: then the map Λ → Z², x ↦ ((x,η),(ζ₀,x)) has image a finite-index subgroup Δ ⊆ Z² of rank 2: contains u = (a,b) and v = (a',b') non-proportional: condition for x = x_u, y = x_v: a·b' = a'·b: so all vectors in Δ satisfy ab' = a'b pairwise: Δ ⊆ {λ(a,b)} ∪-ish: contradiction with rank 2 ⟹ dependent ⟹ η = ±ζ₀ ✓. Section 5 details set. Section 6: 

Theorem 6.1: Let 𝔛, Y as in the statement. Then exists (π, γ) making (𝔛, (π,γ)) a mirror pair [GT24, Def 5.1].

Proof: By Prop 5.1 (cited from [GT24, §4]) applied to the very general H⊕E8²-polarized Y, there is an elliptic fibration π: Y → P^1 with section and an allowable loop γ with the polarization compatible (Def 4.21); by Prop 5.2 the reducible fibers of π are precisely two fibers of type II* with root lattices the two E8-summands, and γ encircles precisely these. It remains to check the mirror-pair conditions of [GT24, Def 5.1]: (a) the polarization lattice of Y is the mirror of the invariant lattice of 𝔛: by Theorem 4.4, K(𝔛) ≅ H⊕E8⊕E8, and by [GT24, §5] the essential lattice of the very general H-polarized Tyurin degeneration 𝔛 is K(𝔛) (with H-summand corresponding to the monodromy-invariant polarization and the E8-summands to the components V_1, V_2, in accordance with the positivity hypothesis, which ensures both components contribute [GT24, §3; ABE22]); by Prop 3.2, H⊕E8⊕E8 is the Dolgachev mirror lattice of H. (b) the monodromy–loop correspondence: the degeneration has Type II monodromy with class ζ ∈ T primitive isotropic; by [GT24, §5, correspondence theorem], for very general data this is equivalent to the existence of a compatible fibration on the mirror with an allowable loop encircling exactly the two reducible fibers — which Prop 5.1–5.2 provide. (c) very generality: 𝔛 by hypothesis (image of 0 outside all NL loci [ABE22]); Y by hypothesis; hence no additional conditions of Def 5.1 remain. By [GT24, Def 5.1 / Thm 5.x], (𝔛, (π, γ)) is a mirror pair. ∎

Add Remark on how the positivity condition is used: (1) ensures the components are RESs with big restriction: places the boundary point in the open Type II stratum [ABE22]; without it, one component could fail to contribute an E8 (e.g., A|_{V_i} could be numerically trivial/fiber-supported), and the mirror fibration would have only one II* fiber — the mirror lattice would then be a proper overlattice/superlattice (e.g., containing ⟨−2n⟩), contradicting the claimed H⊕E8^2; this is why the condition was added in the corrected Definition 3.20 [GT24]. Also (2): the H-quasipolarization compatibility ensures A₀ ≠ fiber-class on components.

Theorem 6.2 ([GT24, §5]). Let 𝔛 → Δ be a very general Tyurin degeneration of K3 surfaces which is H-polarized in the corrected sense, and let (Y, j) be a very general H⊕E8⊕E8-quasipolarized K3 surface. Suppose that Y carries an elliptic fibration ϖ: Y → P^1 with section and an allowable loop γ compatible with j (in the sense of [GT24, Defs. 4.11, 4.21]) such that the reducible fibres of ϖ are precisely two fibres of type II* with root lattices the two E8-summands of j, and γ encircles precisely these two fibres. Then (𝔛, (ϖ, γ)) is a mirror pair in the sense of [GT24, Def. 5.1].
